The proofs came in their fullness, and the answer of the house is stated in the second half of the sentence: then you took the calf, after him, while you were wrongdoers. The then of the verse is the then of the nearness: the calf was taken in the very absence of the prophet who had just come with the proofs, and the taking of the calf is stated with the wronging — the wronging of the souls that the verse of the repentance named.

The verse is the shortest retelling of the calf in the sūrah, and the shortness is the argument: the proofs and the calf stand in one sentence, because the distance between the two is the measure of the house's standing with the proofs. As al-Suddī said, Allah chastised the People of the Book with this verse: the proofs had come in their fullness, and the calf was the answer of the house to the proofs. The story of the calf stands in its full form in the sūrah of the Heights: (7:148 — **“In the absence of Moses, his people made from their ˹golden˺ jewellery an idol of a calf that made a lowing sound. Did they not see that it could neither speak to them nor guide them to the ˹Right˺ Path? Still they took it as a god and were wrongdoers”**). The verse is the standing witness against the claim of the previous verse: the ones who say we believe in what was sent down to us are the ones who took the calf in the absence of the one who brought what was sent down to them — and the calf is the answer of the history to the claim of the creed.

**THE CLEAR SIGNS AND THEIR NUMBER**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the clear signs their list: and Mūsā came to you with the clear signs — that is, he came to you with the clear signs indicating his truth and the soundness of his prophethood: as the staff that became a manifest serpent, and his hand that he brought out white for the beholders, and the splitting of the sea and the becoming of its earth, for him, a dry road, and the locusts and the lice and the frogs, and the rest of the signs that made plain his truth and the soundness of his prophethood. And God named them clear signs only for their being clear, to the ones who look at them, that they are a wonder that no human is able to bring the like of, except by God's subjection of it for him; and it is only the plural of the clear sign, as ṭayyibah and ṭayyibāt. And the meaning of the speech is: Mūsā came to you — O company of the Jews of the Children of Israel — with the signs clear upon his affair, and his truth, and the soundness of his prophethood.

And of His saying, then you took the calf, after him, while you were wrongdoers — God says to them: then you took the calf, after Mūsā, as a god; and the pronoun in His saying, after him, is of the mention of Mūsā; and He said, after Mūsā, only because they took the calf after Mūsā had left them, going to his Lord for his appointment. And it is possible that the pronoun in after him is of the mention of the coming, and the interpretation of the speech then is: Mūsā came to you with the clear signs, then you took the calf, after the coming of the clear signs, while you were wrongdoers — as you say, you came to me and I hated it — meaning, I hated your coming. The verse joins the two proofs — the signs that came, and the calf that was taken after them — and the taking of the calf stands as the house's own answer to the signs, before any argument of the argument.

---

## Verse 2:93

> And when We took your covenant and raised the mountain above you ˹saying˺, “Hold firmly to that ˹Scripture˺ which We have given you and obey,” they answered, “We hear and disobey.” The love of the calf was rooted in their hearts because of their disbelief. Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “How evil is what your ˹so-called˺ belief prompts you to do, if you ˹actually˺ believe ˹in the Torah˺!”

**THE HEARING AND THE DISOBEYING**

The verse returns to the covenant under the mountain, and it gives the answer of the house to the covenant. Remember when We took your covenant and raised above you the Mount: hold firmly to what We have given you, and hear. The covenant of the verse is the covenant of the hearing with the holding, and the answer of the house is the answer of the division: they said, we hear and we disobey. The hearing is affirmed and the obedience is refused, and the two are joined in the one answer, because the hearing without the obedience is the hearing of the argument against the hearer, not the hearing of the acceptance. Qatādah said, in the report of Maʿmar from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, of the calf of the verse: they were made to drink it into their hearts by their disbelief — they absorbed its love, until its love resided in their hearts; and this is also the opinion of Abū al-ʿĀliyah and al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas. The drinking of the calf into the hearts is the deepest picture of the love of the falsehood: the calf did not stand before their eyes only — its love was poured into their hearts, and the hearts drank it with the disbelief.

The verse closes on the command of the Prophet: say, wretched is what your belief commands you, if you are believers. The answer of the verse returns the claim of the belief upon the house: the belief that commands the hearing and the disobeying and the love of the calf is a wretched belief, and the wretchedness is measured by what it commands. The raising of the Mount stands in the sūrah of the Women among the curses of the house: (4:154 — **“We raised the Mount over them ˹as a warning˺ for ˹breaking˺ their covenant”**). The verse is the standing verdict on the religion of the claim: the claim of the belief is answered by the commands of the belief, and the belief that commands the disobedience is not belief — and the house that said we hear and disobey was answered with the wretchedness of what its belief commanded.

**THE HEARING AND ITS LANGUAGE**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its taking: and when We took your covenant, and We raised above you the mountain — remember when We took your covenants, by take what We have given you, of the Torah, which I sent down to you, that you act upon what is in it of My command, and desist from what I forbade you in it — with force, and hear — with seriousness of you in that, and eagerness; and you gave your covenant upon the acting upon that, when We raised above you the mountain.

And as for His saying, and hear — its meaning is, hear what I commanded you with, and accept it by the obedience, as the saying of the man to the man he commands with the command: I heard and I obeyed — meaning, by it, I heard your speech, and I obeyed your command; as the rajaz-poet said: the hearing and the obedience and the submission are the best, and more excusing for the sons of Maym. He means, by his saying, the hearing, the acceptance of what is heard, and the obedience to what is commanded. So likewise the meaning of His saying, and hear, accept what you heard, and act upon it. And the meaning of the verse then is: and when We took your covenant, that take what We have given you with force, and act upon what you heard, and obey God — and We raised above you the mountain for the sake of that.

And Kathīr gives the verse its record: God reminded the Jews of their errors, and their breaking of His covenant, and their transgression and defiance, when He raised Mount Ṭūr above them, so that they would believe and agree to the terms of the covenant; yet they broke it soon afterwards. And of their hearts absorbed the love of the calf — Qatādah said, through ʿAbd al-Razzāq, from Maʿmar: they absorbed its love, until its love resided in their hearts; and this is also the opinion of Abū al-ʿĀliyah and al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas. The hearing of the verse was a hearing with the ear, and the answer of the verse was the disobedience of the heart — and the absorbed love of the calf is the record of what the heart heard in the place of the command.

---

## Verse 2:94

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “If the ˹eternal˺ Home of the Hereafter with Allah is exclusively for you ˹Israelites˺ out of all humanity, then wish for death if what you say is true!”

**THE TEST OF THE WISH**

The verse turns the house's claim upon itself with the test of the wish. Say: if the home of the Hereafter with Allah is purely yours, to the exclusion of all people, then long for death, if you are truthful. The claim of the house was that the Hereafter is theirs alone — that they are the sons of Allah and His beloved, and that none shall enter Paradise except those who are Jews or Christians — and the verse gives the test of the claim: the one whose home is purely his with Allah longs to reach it, and the longing for the home is the longing for death, which is the door of the home. The verse thus measures the truth of the claim by the desire of the claimant: if the Hereafter is truly yours, you should wish to enter it, and the wish for death is the proof of the claim.

The early scholars read the longing as the invocation. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿIkrimah from ʿAbd al-Razzāq, that the meaning is: invoke Allah to bring death upon the lying camp among the two; and in another report from Ibn ʿAbbās, recorded by Ibn Abī Ḥātim through Saʿīd ibn Jubayr: had the Jews asked for death, one of them would have choked on his own saliva — and these reports have authentic chains to Ibn ʿAbbās. Ibn Jarīr recorded in his tafsīr that the Prophet ﷺ said: had the Jews wished for death, they would have died and seen their seats in the Fire; and had those who invoked the curse against the Messenger of Allah ﷺ gone out for the mubāhalah, they would have returned to find no families and no property. The same test stands in the sūrah of al-Jumuʿah: (62:6 — **“Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “O Jews! If you claim to be Allah’s chosen ˹people˺ out of all humanity, then wish for death, if what you say is true.””**). The verse is the standing test of every claim of nearness to Allah: the claim is proved by the longing for the meeting, and the one who refuses the test has confessed the falsehood of his claim.

**THE JUST ISSUE AND THE PROPHET'S WORD**

Al-Ṭabarī gives the verse its place in the argument: this verse is of what God argued with it, for His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ, against the Jews who were between the two sides of the place of his emigration, and by which He disgraced their scholars and their learned — for God commanded His Prophet ﷺ to call them to a just issue between him and them, in what was between him and them of the disagreement; as God commanded him to call the other party, of the Christians — when they differed with him over ʿĪsā and disputed over him — to a deciding between him and them, of the mutual imprecation. And He said to the party of the Jews: if the abode of the Hereafter is yours with God, purely, rather than the people, then long for the death, if you are truthful.

And it reached us that the Messenger of God ﷺ said: had the Jews longed for the death, they would have died, and would have seen their places in the fire; and had those who mutually imprecated with the Messenger of God ﷺ come out, they would have returned finding neither family nor wealth. And Ibn ʿAbbās carries it, through ʿIkrimah, from the Messenger of God ﷺ; and in his other report, from Ibn ʿAbbās, of His saying, then long for the death, if you are truthful: had they longed for the death, one of them would have choked on his own spittle. The verse is the challenge of the wish: the claim of the purity of the abode is put to the test of the longing for the death, and the refusal of the test is recorded as the confession — and the Prophet's word stands over the refusal with its consequence: the death that was not wished would have come with the seeing of the places.

---

## Verse 2:95

> But they will never wish for that because of what their hands have done. And Allah has ˹perfect˺ knowledge of the wrongdoers

**THE REFUSAL AND ITS REASON**

The verse states the answer of the house to the test before the test is given: they will never long for it, because of what their hands have sent before them. The refusal is stated as the standing fact — never — and the reason of the refusal is stated with it: the hands have sent before them what makes the meeting with Allah the thing they cannot wish for. The one who knows what his hands have sent does not long for the standing before the One who received it, and the house knew that it recognized the Prophet and disbelieved in him, and the knowledge is what made the longing impossible. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk reported from Ibn ʿAbbās that the meaning is: since they know that they recognize you and yet disbelieve in you — had they wished for death that day, no Jew would have remained alive on the face of the earth.

The verse closes on the knowledge: and Allah is aware of the wrongdoers. The awareness is the standing answer to the refusal: the ones who refuse the test because of what their hands have sent are known to the One whom they refuse to meet, and the knowledge of the verse is the knowledge that gathers what the hands have sent. The same test was given to the Christians of Najrān when they persisted in the dispute after the knowledge had come: (3:61 — **“say, “Come! Let us gather our children and your children, our women and your women, ourselves and yourselves—then let us sincerely invoke Allah’s curse upon the liars.””**). The verse is the standing proof that the religion of the claim cannot face the meeting: the one whose religion is the claim of the name will never wish for the standing where the names are weighed, and the awareness of Allah stands over the wrongdoers and over what their hands have sent.

**THE NEVER AND THE HANDS THAT SENT**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the never by its cause: this is a report from God of the Jews, and of their hatred of the death, and of their refusing the answer to what they were called to, of the longing for the death — for their knowledge that if they did that, the threat would descend upon them, and the death would alight upon them; and for their knowledge of Muḥammad ﷺ, that he is a messenger from God to them, sent, while they deny him, and that he did not report to them a report but it was truth, as he reported; and they are wary of longing for the death, in fear that the punishment of God alight upon them, by what their hands earned of the sins.

The reports give the never its reading. Ibn Isḥāq carries from Ibn ʿAbbās, through Saʿīd ibn Jubayr or ʿIkrimah: say, if the abode of the Hereafter is yours — that is, call the death upon whichever of the two parties is the more lying; and they refused that upon the Messenger of God ﷺ; and God says to His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ: and they will never long for it, by what their hands sent forward — that is, for their knowledge of what is with them, of the knowledge of you, and the disbelief with that. And al-Ḍaḥḥāk carries from Ibn ʿAbbās: and they will never long for it, ever — he says: O Muḥammad, they will never long for it, ever, because they know that they are liars; and had they been truthful, they would have longed for it, and desired the hastening to My honour; and they will not long for it, ever, by what their hands sent forward. And Ibn Jurayj said: and they were the most fleeing of the people from the death, and they were not to long for it, ever. And of His saying, by what their hands sent forward — He means, by it, what their hands forwarded; and that is only a likeness, upon the like of what the Arabs liken in their speech; and they say to the man who is taken by the offence he committed, or the crime he perpetrated, and is punished for it: this reached you by what your two hands earned, and by what your two hands forwarded — and they add that to the hand, while the crime he perpetrated, for which he deserved the punishment, may have been by the tongue, or by the private part, or by other than that of the members of his body, other than the hand.

---

## Verse 2:96

> You will surely find them clinging to life more eagerly than any other people, even more than polytheists. Each one of them wishes to live a thousand years. But even if they were to live that long, it would not save them from the punishment. And Allah is All-Seeing of what they do

**THE CLINGING TO THE LIFE**

The verse describes the opposite of the longing. You will find them the greediest of people for the life — greedier than the ones who associate partners with Allah, who have no Book and no claim of the Hereafter. The comparison is the argument of the verse: the ones who claim that the Hereafter is purely theirs are found greedier for this life than the ones who do not believe in the Hereafter at all, and the greed is the confession of the heart against the claim of the tongue. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of Ibn Isḥāq, that Allah said to His Prophet: say to them, if the home of the Hereafter with Allah is purely yours to the exclusion of all people, then long for death if you are truthful — meaning, invoke Allah to bring death upon the lying camp among the two; and the Jews declined this offer of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The declining is the proof of the greed: the ones who claim the Hereafter decline the door to it, because the heart is clinging to the life it claims to have left behind.

The verse then gives the measure of the clinging: each one of them loves to be given a life of a thousand years. The thousand is the word of the limit of the wish — the longest life the heart can imagine — and the verse answers the wish with its verdict: it would not remove him from the punishment, should he be given it. The length of the life is not the length of the safety: the one who lives a thousand years stands before the punishment at its end as the one who lives a single day, because the punishment is measured by the deeds, not by the years. The verse closes on the seeing: and Allah is seeing of what they do — the seeing stands over the clinging and the declining and the deeds the hands send before them. The Book states the same standing of the ones who prefer the life: (87:16 — **“But you ˹deniers only˺ prefer the life of this world”**), and the preference of the life is the confession that the Hereafter is not purely theirs.

**THE GREEDIEST OF THE PEOPLE**

Al-Ṭabarī fixes the verse's people: He means by His saying, and you will surely find them the greediest of the people for life — the Jews; he says: O Muḥammad, you will surely find the most intense of the people in greed for the life of this world, and the most intense of them in hatred of the death, are the Jews. And Ibn Isḥāq carries from Ibn ʿAbbās, through Saʿīd ibn Jubayr or ʿIkrimah: and you will surely find them the greediest of the people for life — he means, the Jews; and Abū al-ʿĀliyah said the like, in the reports of al-Rabīʿ; and Mujāhid said the like.

The verse's greed is given its measure by the verse itself: each of them loves that he should be granted a life of a thousand years — and the thousand is the measure that the greed reaches to in the wishing, and the granting of it would not remove him from the punishment, and God is seeing of what they do. The greed of the verse is the answer of the verse before it: the refusal of the longing for the death, and the love of the thousand years, stand together as the two faces of the one heart — the heart that claims the purity of the abode, and clings to the life of the passing. The verse measures the claim by the clinging, and the clinging is the confession that the claim is not sound.

---

## Verse 2:97

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “Whoever is an enemy of Gabriel should know that he revealed this ˹Quran˺ to your heart by Allah’s Will, confirming what came before it—a guide and good news for the believers.”

**THE ENEMY OF THE MESSENGER OF THE REVELATION**

The verse answers the house's last excuse: the enmity of the carrier of the revelation. The scholars of tafsīr agree, as Ibn Jarīr stated, that this verse was revealed about the Jews who claimed that Jibrīl is the enemy of the Jews and that Mīkāʾīl is their friend. The verse answers the claim with the standing of Jibrīl: whoever is the enemy of Jibrīl — then let him know that he brought it down upon your heart, by the permission of Allah, confirming what was before it, a guidance and good news for the believers. The answer is the standing of the carrier: Jibrīl is not the author of the Book but its carrier, and he brought it down by the permission of Allah, and the enmity of the carrier is the enmity of the One who sent him. ʿIkrimah said that Jibr, Mīk and Isrāf all mean servant, while īl means Allah — so the names of the angels are the names of their servanthood, and the enemy of the servant is the enemy of the Master.

Al-Bukhārī records the standing of Jibrīl with the Prophet ﷺ in the report of Anas ibn Mālik: when ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām heard of the arrival of the Prophet ﷺ in Madinah, he came to him and said, I am going to ask you about three things which nobody knows except a prophet — what is the first portent of the Hour, and what is the first meal of the people of Paradise, and why does a child resemble its father or its maternal uncle? — and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: Jibrīl has just informed me of them. The same descent of the Book upon the heart is stated in the sūrah of the Poets: (26:193 — **“which the trustworthy spirit ˹Gabriel˺ brought down”**), and the place of the descent is stated with it: into your heart, that you may be one of the warners. The verse is the standing verdict on the enmity of the carriers of the revelation: the one who makes the carrier of the Book his enemy has made the Book his enemy, because the carrier and the Book come from the One, and the enmity of the messenger is the enmity of the message.

**THE OCCASION OF THE ENMITY**

The people of interpretation agreed, all of them, that this verse came down as an answer to the Jews of the Children of Israel, when they claimed that Jibrīl was their enemy, and that Mīkāʾīl was their patron. Then they differed over the cause for which they said that. Al-Ṭabarī carries the cause of the debate: some said that the cause of their saying that was only the debate that ran between them and the Messenger of God ﷺ, over the affair of his prophethood. And Ibn ʿAbbās carries the scene, in the report of Shahr ibn Ḥawshab: a band of the Jews attended the Messenger of God ﷺ and said: O Abū al-Qāsim, tell us of things we will ask you of, which none knows except a prophet; and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: ask what you will, but make for me the protection of God, and what Yaʿqūb took upon his sons, that if I tell you a thing and you know it, you will follow me upon Islam; and they said: that is for you. And Ibn Isḥāq carries, from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAbd al-Raḥmān ibn Abī Ḥusayn, from Shahr ibn Ḥawshab: that a group of the Jews came to the Messenger of God ﷺ and said: O Muḥammad, tell us of four things we ask you of, and if you do it we will follow you and affirm you and believe in you; and the Messenger of God ﷺ said: upon you is the covenant of God and His pact, that if I inform you of that, you will affirm me? They said: yes.

And Kathīr carries the reading of the names: ʿIkrimah said: Jibr and Mīk and Isrāf all mean worshipper, while īl means God; and Anas ibn Mālik carries the scene of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Salām, who asked the Prophet ﷺ of the three things that none knows except a prophet: what is the first portent of the Hour, and what is the first meal of the people of Paradise, and what makes the child resemble his father or his mother. The verse answers the enmity of the carrier with the descent of the Book: he brought it down upon your heart, by the permission of God — and the heart that received the Book is the place where the enmity of the carrier becomes the enmity of the Book and of the One who sent it down.

---

## Verse 2:98

> Whoever is an enemy of Allah, His angels, His messengers, Gabriel, and Michael, then ˹let them know that˺ Allah is certainly the enemy of the disbelievers

**THE ENMITY THAT REACHES THE LORD**

The verse gathers the enmity of the house into its full compass. Whoever is the enemy of Allah and His angels and His messengers, and Jibrīl and Mīkāʾīl — then Allah is the enemy of the disbelievers. Ibn Jarīr stated that the scholars of tafsīr agree that these two verses were revealed about the Jews who claimed that Jibrīl is the enemy of the Jews and that Mīkāʾīl is their friend; and ʿIkrimah said of the names: Jibr, Mīk and Isrāf all mean servant, while īl means Allah. The verse names Jibrīl and Mīkāʾīl by name although they are included among the angels who are messengers, because the verse was revealed to defend Jibrīl, the emissary between Allah and His prophets, and because the Jews claimed that Jibrīl was their enemy and Mīkāʾīl was their friend; so Allah informed them that whoever is the enemy of one of the two is the enemy of the other and of Allah. The enmity is thus gathered from its ends to its source: the enmity of one angel is the enmity of all the angels, and the enmity of the angels is the enmity of the messengers, and the enmity of the messengers is the enmity of Allah, and the verse closes on the answer of the Lord: Allah is the enemy of the disbelievers. The same standing of the carrier is stated in the sūrah of the Poets: (26:192 — **“This is certainly a revelation from the Lord of all worlds”**), and the trustworthy spirit brought it down upon the heart of the Prophet.

The verse is the standing law of the enmity with Allah: the enmity of the servant with the Lord is the enmity that gathers all the enmities, and the one who takes an enemy from the angels or the messengers has taken Allah as his enemy, because the chain of the revelation is one chain from the Lord to the servant. The verse closes the affair of the house's excuses: the claim of the hearts, the grudging of the grace, the division of the Book, and the enmity of the carrier — and the four excuses stand answered, and the enmity of Allah stands over the disbelievers.

**THE ENMITY THAT GATHERS THE ENEMIES**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse as the gathering of the enmity: this is a report from God, that whoever is an enemy to God, he is an enemy to Him, and an enemy to all His angels and His messengers; and a making known from Him, that whoever is an enemy to Jibrīl is an enemy to Him, and an enemy to Mīkāʾīl, and an enemy to all His angels and His messengers — for the ones whom God named in this verse are the allies of God, and the people of His obedience; and whoever is an enemy to an ally of God is an enemy to God, and has stood out to Him with the waging of war; and whoever is an enemy to God, God is an enemy to him.

And he carries the report of the saying: the Prophet ﷺ asked the Jews, and said: I ask you, by your Book that you read, do you find in it that ʿĪsā the son of Maryam gave you the good news of the coming of a messenger whose name is Aḥmad? And they said: O God, we found You in our Book, but we hated you, because You make lawful the wealth and pour out the blood; and God sent down: whoever is an enemy to God, and His angels, and His messengers, and Jibrīl, and Mīkāl — then indeed, God is an enemy to the disbelievers. The verse gathers the enmity into one: the enmity of one of the allies is the enmity of all of them, and the enmity of all of them is the enmity of God — and the closing names the disbelievers rather than the ones who named the enmity, for the enmity of the verse is the disbelief itself.

---

## Verse 2:99

> Indeed, We have sent down to you ˹O Prophet˺ clear revelations. ˹But˺ none will deny them except the rebellious

**THE CLEAR SIGNS AND THE REBELLIOUS**

The verse states the standing of what has been sent down. We have sent down to you manifest signs, and none disbelieve in them except the rebellious. Ibn Jarīr said: We have sent to you, O Muhammad, clear signs that testify to your prophethood — and these signs are contained in the Book of Allah, which narrates the secrets of the knowledge the Jews possess and hide, and the stories of their earlier generations, and the texts of their Books that they altered. The clarity of the signs is the standing of the revelation: the signs are manifest in themselves, and the Book carries within it the proofs of the prophethood — the knowledge of the hidden of the People of the Book and the news of the generations before. The verse then states the standing of the ones who refuse the manifest: none disbelieve in them except the fāsiqūn — the ones who have departed from the obedience of Allah, and the departure is what stands between them and the acceptance of the manifest.

The verse is the answer of the Lord to the excuses of the house: the signs are manifest, and the refusal of the manifest is not a doubt but a rebellion, and the rebellion is the standing that the sūrah has named from its beginning — the fāsiqūn are the ones whom Allah leaves to stray by the parable of the gnat, and they are the ones who disbelieve in the manifest signs. The same standing of the signs is stated in the sūrah of the Spider: (29:49 — **“But this ˹Quran˺ is ˹a set of˺ clear revelations ˹preserved˺ in the hearts of those gifted with knowledge. And none denies Our revelations except the ˹stubborn˺ wrongdoers”**). The verse closes the affair of the revelation upon the two standings: the clarity of the signs and the rebellion of the deniers, and the two stand together as the standing argument of the sūrah.

**THE SIGNS AND THE SCENE OF IBN ṢŪRIYĀ**

Al-Ṭabarī reads the signs by their content: and We have sent down to you clear signs — that is, We sent down to you, O Muḥammad, clear marks indicating your prophethood; and those signs are what the Book of God, which He sent down to Muḥammad ﷺ, contained, of the hidden sciences of the Jews, and the treasured secrets of their reports, and the reports of their ancients of the Children of Israel, and the telling of what their Books contained, which none knew except their scholars and their learned — and what their ancients and their later ones changed, and altered, of their rulings that were in the Torah; and God brought them out in the Book He sent down upon His Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ. And al-Ḍaḥḥāk carries from Ibn ʿAbbās: and We have sent down to you clear signs — he says: you recite it upon them, and inform them of it, morning and evening and between that, while you are, with them, unlettered, who never read a Book, and you inform them of what is in their hands, upon its face; God says: in that, for them, is a lesson and a making plain, and upon them an argument, if they knew.

And Ibn Isḥāq gives the verse its scene, through ʿIkrimah and Saʿīd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn ʿAbbās: Ibn Ṣūriyā al-Fiṭyūnī said to the Messenger of God ﷺ — and the report is carried in the two ways. And al-Ṭabarī reads the closing: and none disbelieves in them except the rebellious — and none denies them; and the interpretation of the verse is: We have sent down to you, in what We revealed to you of the Book, clear marks making plain to the scholars of the Children of Israel and their learned — who deny your prophethood and deny your message — that you are a messenger to them from Me, and a prophet sent; and none denies those signs, which indicate your truth and your prophethood, which I sent down to you in My Book, and disbelieves in them, of them, except the one who leaves, of them, his religion, and leaves, of them, My obligations upon him, in the Book he professes the affirmation of; and as for the one who holds fast, of them, to his religion, and follows, of them, the ruling of his Book, he affirms what I sent down to you of My signs — and they are the ones who believed.

---

## Verse 2:100

> Why is it that every time they make a covenant, a group of them casts it aside? In fact, most of them do not believe

**THE COVENANTS CAST ASIDE**

The verse asks the question of the house's covenants. Is it not that every time they make a covenant, a party of them cast it aside? The question is the question of the wonder: the covenant is taken, and the taking is followed by the casting, and the casting is the standing habit of the house with its covenants. This is how al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī read the verse of the casting, as the standing of the promise: there is not a promise they make but they break it and abandon it — they make a promise today and break it tomorrow. The verse then states the root of the casting: nay, most of them do not believe. The casting of the covenant is the fruit of the absence of the belief: the covenant is kept by the belief that stands behind it, and the one who does not believe in the One who took the covenant will cast it aside when the keeping of it crosses his desire.

The verse is the gathering of the sūrah's argument with the house into one verdict: the covenants were taken — the covenant of the mountain, the covenant of the commandments, the covenant of the blood and the homes — and the casting followed every taking. The sūrah has recounted the takings one by one and the turnings one by one, and the verse gathers them into the one habit: every time they make a covenant, a party of them cast it aside. The verse is the standing warning to every community that takes a covenant with Allah: the covenant is kept by the belief, and the casting is the fruit of the absence of the belief — and most of them do not believe is the measure of the house and the warning of the community that reads it. The same covenant cast behind the back stands in the sūrah of the Family of ʿImrān: (3:187 — **“when Allah took the covenant of those who were given the Scripture to make it known to people and not hide it, yet they cast it behind their backs and traded it for a fleeting gain. What a miserable profit”**).

**THE CASTING AND ITS OCCASION**

Kathīr gives the verse its occasion: when the Messenger of God ﷺ was sent, and God reminded the Jews of the covenant they had with Him, especially concerning Muḥammad ﷺ, Mālik ibn al-Ṣayf said: by God, God never made a covenant with us about Muḥammad, nor did He take a pledge from us at all; and God sent down: is it not that every time they make a covenant, a party of them cast it aside? And al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī read the verse of the casting as the standing of the promise: nay, most of them do not believe — there is not a promise they make, but they break it and abandon it; they make a promise today, and break it tomorrow.

The verse is the sūrah's summing of the house's standing with the covenant: the covenant of the mountain, and the covenant of the blood, and the covenant of the Book — and the casting is the one answer of the many. The party that casts is named a party, because the casting is not the deed of the house all of it — and the nay, most of them do not believe stands over the casting as its measure: the casting is the fruit of the absence of the belief, and the belief that is not is the root of the covenant that is not kept.

---

## Verse 2:101

> Now, when a messenger from Allah has come to them—confirming their own Scriptures—some of the People of the Book cast the Book of Allah behind their backs as if they did not know

**THE COMING THAT WAS ANSWERED WITH A CASTING**

The sūrah has told of this coming once before, when it said of the house that they used to pray for victory by the Prophet over the polytheists, and that when the Book they recognized came, confirming their Scripture, they rejected it (2:89 — **“when there came to them a Book from Allah which they recognized, confirming the Scripture they had ˹in their hands˺, they rejected it”**). Here the sūrah returns to the same moment, and fixes its people. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its names: God means by His saying, now when there came to them, the scholars of the Jews and their learned, of the Children of Israel, and by the messenger He means Muḥammad ﷺ; and al-Suddī said: when there came to them Muḥammad ﷺ. The confirming of the verse runs in both directions: Muḥammad ﷺ confirms the Torah, and the Torah confirms him, in that he is a prophet of God sent to His creation. What came to them was not therefore a stranger and not a rival Book, but what their own Book had described; and the casting of the verse is the answer of the scholars to the confirming. God informs that the Jews, when there came to them the Messenger of God ﷺ from God with the confirmation of what was in their hands of the Torah — that Muḥammad ﷺ is a prophet of God — a party cast: they denied him and rejected him, after they had been affirming him, in envy of him and aggression upon him. The party is named by what it was given: of those who were given the Book — the scholars whom God gave the knowledge of the Torah and what is in it; and the Book they cast is the Torah itself.

**BEHIND THE BACKS**

The casting is read by the idiom of the tongue. Al-Ṭabarī gives the proverb its shape: and behind their backs — they made it behind their backs; and this is a likeness, said of everyone who rejects a matter he had been upon his mind of: so-and-so has made this matter of his upon the back, and made it behind his back — he turned from it, and turned aside, and went away. The image is of a Book not lost but thrown, and thrown by the ones who know what they throw. Al-Suddī gives the scene: when there came to them Muḥammad ﷺ, they opposed him with the Torah and disputed with him by it, and the Torah and the Qurʾān agreed — so they cast the Torah, and took to the book of Āṣif, and the magic of Hārūt and Mārūt; and that is the saying of God, as if they did not know. And Qatādah said: a party of those who were given the Book broke the Book of God behind their backs, as if they did not know — the people knew, but they spoiled their knowledge, and denied, and disbelieved, and concealed. Al-Qurṭubī reads the verse's grammar upon the same face: confirming is an attribute of the messenger, and it may be installed upon the state; and the Book intended is the Torah, because their disbelief in the Prophet ﷺ and their denial of him is a casting of it.

**THE CASTING OF THE ONES WHO KNOW**

Two sayings give the casting its strangest face, for a thing may be cast behind the back by rejection, and it may be cast behind the back by honor. Al-Shaʿbī said: they used to read the Torah, and they did not work by it. And Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah said: they rolled it in the silk and the brocade, and adorned it with the gold and the silver — the Book honored in its wrapping and abandoned in its command. Al-Saʿdī gathers the consequence: they were claiming that they were holding fast to their Book; and when they disbelieved in this Messenger and in what he came with, they cast the Book of God that was sent down to them, and threw it away in aversion from it — and their disbelief in this Messenger became a disbelief in their Book, so there remained nothing in their hands. The as if they did not know stands therefore as the witness of the verse against them: it is an informing from God that they denied the truth upon knowledge of it and acquaintance, and that they opposed the command of God knowing its obligation upon them — the ignorance of the verse is the ignorance of the acting, not of the knowing.

---

## Verse 2:102

> They ˹instead˺ followed the magic promoted by the devils during the reign of Solomon. Never did Solomon disbelieve, rather the devils disbelieved. They taught magic to the people, along with what had been revealed to the two angels, Hârût and Mârût, in Babylon. The two angels never taught anyone without saying, “We are only a test ˹for you˺, so do not abandon ˹your˺ faith.” Yet people learned ˹magic˺ that caused a rift ˹even˺ between husband and wife; although their magic could not harm anyone except by Allah’s Will. They learned what harmed them and did not benefit them—although they already knew that whoever buys into magic would have no share in the Hereafter. Miserable indeed was the price for which they sold their souls, if only they knew

**THE ONES WHO FOLLOWED, AND WHAT THEY FOLLOWED**

The casting of the verse before was not into a void: the hand that threw the Book reached for something else. Al-Ṭabarī reads the verse by its people: God means by His saying, and they followed what the devils recite, the party of the scholars of the Jews and their learned, whom God described as casting His Book behind their backs — they rejected His Book, which they know is sent down from Him upon His Prophet ﷺ, and broke His covenant, and preferred the magic that the devils recited in the kingdom of Sulaymān the son of Dāwūd, and followed it; and that is the loss and the plain error. The people of interpretation differed over the ones intended: some said God intended the Jews who were in the days of the emigration of the Messenger of God ﷺ, because they disputed with him by the Torah and found the Torah agreeing with the Qurʾān, commanding the following of Muḥammad ﷺ and the affirming of him, so they disputed instead by the books the people had copied from the soothsayers in the days of Sulaymān; and others said God intended the Jews of the days of Sulaymān. Al-Ṭabarī decides: it is a reproach from God of the scholars of the Jews who reached the Messenger of God ﷺ and denied his prophethood while they knew that he was for God a messenger sent — for the affair of the magic did not cease to be in the Jews from the days of Sulaymān until God sent His Prophet with the truth, and the following of the forefathers is ascribed to the descendants.

**THE SEATS OF THE LISTENING**

The reciting of the devils is given its story in the reports. Al-Suddī said: the devils used to ascend to the heaven and sit in it in the seats of the listening, and hear of the speech of the angels of what will be in the earth, of death or rain or affair; and they came to the soothsayers and informed them, and the soothsayers told the people, and they found it as they said; until when the soothsayers trusted them, they lied for them and brought in other than it, and added with every word seventy words; and the people wrote that talk in the books, and it spread in the Children of Israel that the jinn know the unseen; and Sulaymān sent among the people and gathered those books. Mujāhid said: the devils used to hear the revelation, and for the word they heard they added two hundred of its like; and Sulaymān sent to what they had written of that and gathered it; and when Sulaymān died, the devils found it and taught it the people, and it is the magic. And Ibn ʿAbbās carried the same to the man who came from Kūfah: the devils used to steal the listening from the heaven, and one of them came with a word of truth he had heard, and when he was trusted in it he mixed with it seventy lies, and the hearts of the people drank it; and God acquainted Sulaymān upon it, and he buried it. And Qatādah said: it reached us that the devils invented a book in which was magic and a great matter, and they spread it among the people and taught them it; and when Sulaymān heard of that, he followed those books and buried them under his throne, in dislike that the people should learn them; and when God took His prophet Sulaymān, the devils brought them out from their place and taught them the people, and informed them that this was a knowledge Sulaymān used to keep for himself; and God excused His prophet Sulaymān and cleared him, when He said: and Sulaymān did not disbelieve, but the devils disbelieved.

**UNDER THE THRONE**

Saʿīd ibn Jubayr gives the exoneration its scene: Sulaymān used to follow what was in the hands of the devils, of the magic, and take it and bury it under his throne, in the house of his treasure, and the devils were not able to reach it; and they came near to the men and said: do you wish the knowledge by which Sulaymān used to subdue the devils and the winds and other than that? They said: yes. They said: it is in the house of his treasure, and under his throne. And the men stirred it and brought it out, and worked by it; and the people of the Ḥijāz said: Sulaymān used to work by this, and this is magic! And God sent down, upon the tongue of His prophet Muḥammad ﷺ, the exoneration of Sulaymān. And Ibn Isḥāq carries the forging itself: the devils, when they knew the death of Sulaymān the son of Dāwūd, wrote the kinds of the magic — whoever loves to reach such and such, let him do such and such — and made it in a book, and sealed it with a seal upon the engraving of the seal of Sulaymān, and wrote in its title: this is what Āṣaf the son of Barkhiyā, the truthful, wrote for the king Sulaymān the son of Dāwūd, of the treasures of the stores of knowledge; then they buried it under his throne; and the remnants of the Children of Israel brought it out, and when they came upon it they said: Sulaymān the son of Dāwūd was nothing but by this! And they spread the magic among the people. Al-Qurṭubī gives the exoneration its doctrine: God's saying, and Sulaymān did not disbelieve, is an exoneration from God of Sulaymān — no one had ascribed the disbelief to him in the verse before, but the Jews ascribed the magic to him; and since the magic is disbelief, it became in the place of the one who ascribes disbelief to him. And the people of the Sunnah went to that the magic is fixed and has a reality, while the generality of the Muʿtazilah went to that the magic has no reality, and it is only a delusion and an imagining and a kind of lightness and sleight — as in (20:66 — **“their ropes and staffs appeared to him—by their magic—to be slithering”**).

**THE TWO ANGELS AND THE TRIAL**

Mujāhid carries the story of the two angels: the angels were astonished at the wronging of the children of Ādam, while the messengers and the books and the clear signs had come to them; and their Lord said to them: choose of you two angels, whom I will send down to judge in the earth between the children of Ādam; and they chose Hārūt and Mārūt; and He said to them, when He sent them down: you were astonished at the children of Ādam, and at their wronging and their disobedience, while the messengers and the books come to them from behind behind, and between Me and you there is no messenger — so do thus and thus; and He commanded them with a command and forbade them; and they came down upon that, and there was none more obedient to God than they two; and they judged and were just, judging the day between the children of Ādam, and ascending in the evening to be with the angels — until there was sent down to them al-Zuharah, in the most beautiful image of a woman, seeking judgment. Al-Qurṭubī gives Bābil its faces: it is a region of the earth; and Ibn Masʿūd said to the people of Kūfah: you are between al-Ḥīrah and Bābil; and Qatādah said: it is from Naṣībīn to the head of the spring; and a people said it is in the west, and this is weak.

The trial of the two stands in their warning: they teach not anyone until they say, we are only a trial, so do not disbelieve. Al-Suddī carries what follows the warning: when the man came to the two seeking to learn, they counseled him — do not fall into disbelief, we are a trial; and when he refused their counsel, they said: go to that pile of ashes, and urinate upon it; and when he urinated upon it, the light — the light of the belief — departed from him, shining until it entered the heaven, and there descended upon him something black, like smoke, and entered him. And it is reported that a man came upon the two seeking to learn the magic from them, and found them hung by their feet, their eyes blue, their skins blackened, nothing between their tongues and the water but four fingers, punished with the thirst.

**THE SHARE AND THE PRICE**

What is learned of the two is named by its fruit: Qatādah said of they learn of the two of them what they separate by, between the man and his wife: their separating is the binding of each of the two from his companion, and the making hateful of each of the two to his companion. And the harm is held within its limit: al-Ṭabarī reads, and they are not harming by it anyone except by the permission of God — the ones who learn, of the two angels Hārūt and Mārūt, what they separate by between the man and his wife, are not harming, by what they learned of that, anyone of the people, except the one God decreed upon that it harms him; and the permission here is in the meaning of the knowledge — except by what preceded in the knowledge of God that it harms him. The verse then closes the account of the exchange: Qatādah said of they knew that whoever purchased it, for him is not in the Hereafter a share: the People of the Book knew, in the covenant of God to them, that the sorcerer has no share with God on the Day of Resurrection; and al-Suddī said: the Jews knew that the one who learns it, or chooses it, has not in the Hereafter a share. And the share is read by its faces: Mujāhid said, a portion; and al-Suddī the like; and Sufyān the like; and Qatādah said: he has not in the Hereafter an argument; and al-Ḥasan said: he has not a religion; and Ibn ʿAbbās said: a subsistence. And the lām of His saying, for the one who purchased it, is the lām of the oath, and the speech is in the meaning of the oath. And God answers the question of how He said, if they knew, after He said, and they knew: it is of the delayed whose meaning is the advanced; and it was said: He negated from them the knowledge after describing them with the knowing, because they did not act upon what they knew — and the one who knows is only the one who acts upon his knowledge, and when his act contradicts his knowledge, he is in the meanings of the ignorant.

---

## Verse 2:103

> If only they were faithful and mindful ˹of Allah˺, there would have been a better reward from Allah, if only they knew

**THE CONDITION THAT DID NOT HAPPEN**

The verse returns from the story of the magic to the measure, and the measure is short. Al-Ṭabarī reads it by its people: God means by His saying, and if they believed and were God-fearing — if the ones who learn from the two angels what they separate by, between the man and his wife, believed, and affirmed God and His Messenger and what he brought them of what is from their Lord, and were God-fearing of their Lord, and feared His punishment, and obeyed Him by the paying of His obligations and the avoiding of His disobediences — the recompense of God of them, and His rewarding of them for their belief in Him and their fear of Him, would be better for them than the magic and what they earned by it; and He negated by His saying, if they knew, the knowledge from them: that they be knowing the amount of the reward of God, and the measure of His recompense upon His obedience. Al-Qurṭubī reads the fear by its object: and they were God-fearing — He means, they feared the magic. The two conditions stand unfulfilled, and the verse sets the unfulfilled against the done: they learned what harms and does not benefit, and the better remained with God, unclaimed.

**THE RETURNING OF THE REWARD**

The mathūbah is given its root in the tongue: it is a source from the saying of the speaker, I rewarded you, a rewarding, and a thawāb, and a mathūbah; and the root of that is from, the thing returned to you, in the meaning of, came back; and it is said, I returned it to you — I returned it to you, and gave it back; and the meaning of the rewarding of the man the man, upon the gift and other than it, is his returning to him of it a substitute, and his giving back to him of it a compensation; then every one who compensates another, for his act, or his gift, or a hand of his that preceded from him to him, was made a rewarder of him; and from it is the thawāb of God to His servants upon their acts, in the meaning of His giving them the compensation and the recompense upon it, until there returns to them a substitute of the act they did for Him. And the people of interpretation said with this: Qatādah said, of His saying, a mathūbah from God: a thawāb from God; and al-Suddī said: as for the mathūbah, it is the thawāb; and al-Rabīʿ said: a thawāb from God.

The grammarians of Baṣrah read the structure of the verse upon two faces. Some of them held that it is of what is sufficed, by the indication of the speech upon its meaning, from the mention of its answer — its meaning is, if they believed and were God-fearing, they would be rewarded, but it was dispensed with, by the indication of the report of the mathūbah, from his saying, they would be rewarded; and al-Akhfash Saʿīd said: there is not for the law here an answer in the wording, but there is in the meaning, and the meaning is, they would be rewarded. And some of the grammarians of Baṣrah denied that, and held that the answer of His saying, and if they believed and were God-fearing, is the mathūbah, and that the law was answered by the mathūbah, for the nearness of its meaning to the meaning of the lāʾin, in that they are two recompenses and the two answers of the belief. The verse leaves the mathūbah standing against the magic: the earning of the devils passes away, and (16:96 — **“Whatever you have will end, but whatever Allah has is everlasting”**), and the returning of the substitute is the name of the reward that remains.

---

## Verse 2:104

> O believers! Do not say, “Râ’ina.” [Herd us!] But say, “Unẓurna,” [Tend to us!] and listen ˹attentively˺. And the disbelievers will suffer a painful punishment

**THE WORD THE BELIEVERS USED TO SAY**

The address turns from the house of Israel to the believers, in their own tongue, and the first thing it takes from them is a word. The Muslims used to say to the Prophet ﷺ: rāʿinā — of the murāʿāh, meaning, give us your hearing, and empty your hearing for our speech; one says of it: he inclined to the thing, and he tended it, and he tended it mutually — he inclined to it and listened. Al-Ṭabarī gives the forbidding its reason: God advanced to the believers with the restraining of them, from saying to him, of the speech, what is in it rudeness, and commanded them to choose, for his address, of the words, their finest, and of the meanings, their tenderest. And of that was their saying, rāʿinā, for what is in it of the bearing of the meaning: tend us, we tend you — since the mutual form is not but between two, as the speaker says: give us, and converse with us, and sit with us, in the meaning of, do with us, and we do with you; and it bears too the meaning of, give us your hearing, until we make you understand, and you understand from us. And God forbade the Companions of Muḥammad from saying that so, and from the singling of their asking of him, with the waiting of them and the respite of them, that they understand from him, with a reverencing of him and a magnifying of him; and that they not ask him what they asked of that, upon the face of the rudeness and the frowning, nor with the harshness and the coarseness, in imitation of the Jews in their address of the prophet of God ﷺ, and their saying to him: hear, not hearing, and rāʿinā.

**THE DOOR OF THE INSULT**

The reports give the word its history. Mujāhid said: do not say rāʿinā — do not say a contradiction; and in his other report: do not say, hear from us, and we hear from you. And as for the saying reported of ʿAṭāʾ, that rāʿinā was a word of the Jews, in the meaning of the insult and the mockery, and the believers used it, taking it from them — al-Ṭabarī states that it is not permissible, in the description of the believers, that they take, of the speech of the people of association, a speech they do not know the meaning of, then use it between them, and in the address of their prophet ﷺ. But it is possible that it be of what was reported of Qatādah: that it was a sound word, understood, of the speech of the Arabs, agreeing with a word of the speech of the Jews in other than the Arabic tongue — which is with the Jews an insult, and is with the Arabs, give me your hearing, and empty it, that you may understand from me; and God knew the meaning of the Jews, in their saying that to the Prophet ﷺ, and that their meaning in it is other than its meaning in the speech of the Arabs, so He forbade the believers the saying of it to the Prophet ﷺ, lest the one whose meaning in it is other than the meaning of the believers dare to address the Messenger of God ﷺ by it.

Al-Suddī gives the door its opener: Rifāʿah ibn Zayd, a Jewish man of the tribe of Qaynuqāʿ, used to come to the Prophet ﷺ and say to him: hear, not hearing — and the Muslims used to think that the prophets are greeted and honored with this type of speech, and this is why some of them used to say it. And the Jews had found in the word of the believers their occasion: it was said that rāʿinā is of the ruʿūnah — when they wished to make a man foolish, they said to him, rāʿinā, in the meaning of, O foolish one; and when the Jews heard this word from the Muslims, they said between themselves: we used to insult Muḥammad secretly, so make it with him openly — and they used to come to him and say: rāʿinā, O Muḥammad, and laugh between themselves. It was mentioned too that the reading of Ibn Masʿūd was: do not say rāʿūnā, in the meaning of a matter sound for a group, by their tending.

**UNZURNA AND THE LISTENING**

The verse gives the believers their word: and say unẓurnā — Mujāhid said: make us understand, make plain to us, O Muḥammad. Al-Qurṭubī reads the command: they were commanded to address him ﷺ with the reverence, and the meaning is: turn to us, and look at us. And it was read, anẓurnā, with the cutting of the hamzah, in the meaning of, delay us — as in His saying: (38:79 — **“Then delay my end until the Day of their resurrection”**); and al-Ṭabarī said: there is no face for the reading of that so, in this place. The Book itself records the word of the house that made the change necessary, in the sūrah of the Women: (4:46 — **“playing with words and discrediting the faith”**). And the closing of the verse sets the two standings together: the painful punishment of the disbelievers, and the chosen word of the believers — for the tongue of the address of the Prophet ﷺ is of the religion, and the insult that hides in a word is not hidden from the One who sent down the verse.

---

## Verse 2:105

> The disbelievers from the People of the Book and the polytheists would not want you to receive any blessing from your Lord, but Allah selects whoever He wills for His mercy. And Allah is the Lord of infinite bounty

**THE WISHING THAT SENDS NOTHING DOWN**

The verse opens the sūrah's telling of what the two houses carry toward this community, and it begins with the wishing. Al-Ṭabarī reads it by its language: God means by His saying, they do not wish — they do not love; one says of it, so-and-so wished such a thing, he wishes it, a wishing, and a love. And the polytheists are in the place of the genitive, by the joining upon the People of the Book, and the meaning of the speech is: the ones who disbelieved, of the People of the Book, do not love, nor the polytheists, that there be sent down upon you of the good, from your Lord. The interpretation is therefore: the disbelievers, of the People of the Book, do not love, nor the polytheists, the worshippers of the idols, that there be sent down upon you of the good that was with God, and He sent down upon you; and the polytheists, and the disbelievers of the People of the Book, wished that God not send down upon you the Criterion, and what He revealed to Muḥammad ﷺ of His wisdom and His signs. The Jews loved that, and their followers of the polytheists — only in envy of them, and aggression upon the believers. The wishing of the verse is thus the measure of the envy: it is not the wishing of a possession, but the wishing of the withholding, the wishing that the revelation itself not come down.

**THE RULING THE VERSE DRAWS**

Al-Ṭabarī draws from the verse its ruling for the community: in this verse is a plain indication that God Most High forbade the believers from the leaning to their enemies, of the People of the Book and the polytheists, and the listening to their speech, and the accepting of anything they come to them with, upon the face of the sincere counsel to them — by His acquainting them with what the People of the Book and the polytheists hide for them, of the malice and the envy, though they show with their tongues other than what they hide. The verse is read, upon this, as the community's guard upon its counsel: the one whose hidden is the envy and the malice is not taken a counselor, however fair his shown tongue.

**THE SINGLING OF THE MERCY**

The answer of the verse to the wishing is the singling: and God singles out, with His mercy, whom He wills. Al-Ṭabarī reads it: God singles out whom He wills, with His prophethood and His message, and sends him to whom He wills of His creation, and bestows with the belief upon whom He loves of them, and guides him to it; and His singling out of them with it is His making them single with it, other than the rest of His creation. And God made His message, to whom He sent it of His creation, and His guidance of whom He guided of His servants, a mercy from Him to him — to bring him by it to His satisfaction and His love, and his winning by it the Garden, and his deserving by it His praise; and all of that is a mercy from God to him. And His saying, and God is the Lord of infinite bounty, is a report that every good that reaches the servants, in their religion and their world, is from Him, in beginning and in grace, without any deserving of theirs preceding it. The sūrah of Āl ʿImrān states the same singling in the same wording: (3:74 — **“He chooses whoever He wills to receive His mercy. And Allah is the Lord of infinite bounty”**). The good the two houses wish withheld is the mercy the Giver singles — and the envy of the verse is, at its root, the denial of the singling.

---

## Verse 2:106

> If We ever abrogate a verse or cause it to be forgotten, We replace it with a better or similar one. Do you not know that Allah is Most Capable of everything

**THE OBJECTION AND ITS OCCASION**

The Jews of the city raised, against the Messenger ﷺ, the changing of the rulings, and the verse answers them by the root of the changing itself. Al-Alūsī carries the occasion: the verse was sent down when the polytheists — or the Jews — said: do you not see to Muḥammad ﷺ, commanding his Companions with a command, then forbidding them from it and commanding them with its contrary, and saying today a saying and returning from it tomorrow — this Qurʾān is nothing but the speech of Muḥammad, saying it of himself. The objection was therefore an objection to the revelation by the changing of the revelation, and the verse answers it by establishing that the changing is of God, and that it is of His power.

Al-Ṭabarī gives the abrogation its root in the tongue: the root of the naskh is from the transcribing of the book, and it is the moving of it, from a copy, to another other than it; and so the meaning of the abrogating of the ruling, to another, is only the transferring of it, and the moving of its expression, to other than it. Al-Baghawī gathers the two faces of the word: the naskh, in the language, is two things — one of them in the meaning of the transferring and the moving, and from it is the transcribing of the book, that it be moved from a book to a book; and upon this face, all of the Qurʾān is abrogated, for it was transcribed from the Preserved Tablet; and the second is in the meaning of the lifting — one says of it, the sun abrogated the shadow, meaning, it went with it and annulled it. Ibn Kathīr fixes the domain of the word: the naskh occurs only with the commandments, and the prohibitions, and the permissions; and as for the stories, they do not undergo the naskh.

**THE LETTER AND THE RULING**

When that is the meaning of the abrogating of the verse, it is equal — when its ruling is abrogated, and its obligation changed and exchanged, and the obligation of the servants moved from what was incumbent upon them by it — whether its writing is affirmed and left, or its trace is erased and forgotten, since it is, in both of its states, abrogated; and the ruling that occurs, by which the first ruling is exchanged, and to which the obligation of the servants is transitioned, is the abrogating. The Companions of ʿAbd Allāh ibn Masʿūd said: whatever We abrogate, of a verse — We affirm its writing, and exchange its ruling; and Mujāhid said the like, of the Companions of Ibn Masʿūd; and the like was reported of Abū al-ʿĀliyah, and of Muḥammad ibn Kaʿb al-Quraẓī. Al-Baghawī gives the face its example: the letter stands, and the ruling is abrogated, as with the verse of the bequest to the relatives, and the verse of the waiting of the widowhood. And Qatādah gives the abrogating its form: God was abrogating the verse, by the verse after it; and the prophet of God ﷺ recited the verse, or more than that, then it was forgotten and raised.

**THE FORGETTING AND THE DEFERRING**

Al-Ṭabarī states the worthiest reading of His saying, or We cause it to be forgotten: the one who read it, or We defer it, in the meaning of, We leave it — for God informed His Prophet ﷺ that whatever ruling He exchanged or changed, or did not exchange and did not change, He is the bringer of better than it, or of its like; and the worthiest with the verse, when that is its meaning, is that the report of what He does, when He changes and exchanges the ruling of a verse, be followed by the report of what He does, when He does not exchange that and does not change. And Mujāhid read the other face: or We defer it — We defer it, and We delay it; and ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr read it: We defer it. A people, meanwhile, denied the reading of the one who read, or We forget it, when it is intended by it the forgetting; and they said: it is not permissible that the Messenger of God ﷺ forget, of the Qurʾān, anything of what is not abrogated, except that he forget a thing of it, then remember it; and they said: if he had forgotten a thing of it, it would not have been permissible, upon the ones who read it and memorized it of his Companions, that they forget it all; and they said: in the saying of God, (17:86 — **“We could have certainly taken away what We have revealed to you”**), is what tells that God did not make His prophet forget, of what He brought him of the knowledge, anything. The verse gathers the two faces of the change — the abrogation that exchanges the ruling, and the forgetting or the deferring that stays the recitation — and the bringing of the better or the like stands over the two: the One who abrogates is the One who knows the better, and the knowing of His power, at the close of the verse, is the ground of the submitting.

---

## Verse 2:107

> Do you not know that the kingdom of the heavens and the earth belongs ˹only˺ to Allah, and you have no guardian or helper besides Allah

**THE REPORTS OF THE LIFTING**

The verse follows the abrogation with the reports of its faces. Al-Ḥasan said of His saying, whatever We abrogate, of a verse, or We cause it to be forgotten, We bring better than it: your prophet ﷺ was recited a Qurʾān, then he forgot it, and it was nothing; and of the Qurʾān is what has been abrogated, while you recite it. Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: whatever We abrogate, of a verse — He says, whatever We exchange, of a verse; and he said, of the other reading: or We leave it — We leave it, and do not exchange it. Qatādah said: God was forgetting His prophet ﷺ what He willed, and abrogating what He willed. And Mujāhid carries from ʿUbayd ibn ʿUmayr: or We cause it to be forgotten — We raise it from you; and al-Rabīʿ said: We raise it; and God Most High sent down matters of the Qurʾān, then raised them.

Qatādah gives the lifting its standing example, from Anas ibn Mālik: those seventy of the Anṣār who were killed at Biʾr Maʿūnah — we read, with them and of them, a book: convey, from us, to our people, that we met our Lord, and He was pleased with us, and pleased us; and then that was raised. The abrogation is read, by this report, not as a loss but as a lifting: the words of the martyrs were carried up with them.

**THE KINGDOM THAT ANSWERS**

The verse answers the objection of the abrogation with the kingdom. Al-Ṭabarī reads the address: do you not know — and the speech, though its apparent is the address of the Prophet ﷺ, is intended for his Companions; and the proof of that is the indication of the three verses after it — and you have not, other than God, a guardian or a helper, and or do you intend to ask of your messenger. Al-Qurṭubī gives the kingdom its content: His is the kingdom of the heavens and the earth — by the bringing into being and the creating, and the dominion and the authority, and the executing of the command and the will. Al-Alūsī reads the knowledge the verse asks: you have known, O addressed one, that God Most High has the overpowering authority and the manifest mastery, necessitating the complete power, upon the disposing of the whole, in the bringing into being and the annihilating, and the commanding and the forbidding, according to what His will requires — there is no opposer to His command, and no reviewer of His judgment. The sūrah of the Table states the same kingdom in the same wording: (5:120 — **“To Allah ˹alone˺ belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth and everything within. And He is Most Capable of everything”**). The one who objects to the abrogation is answered, then, by the kingdom he stands in: the owner of the kingdom does what He wills, in the rulings as in the creation — and the absence of the guardian and the helper, at the close of the verse, is the reminder that the objector has no one to protect him from the Owner of what he objects to.

---

## Verse 2:108

> Or do you ˹believers˺ intend to ask of your Messenger as Moses was asked before? But whoever trades belief for disbelief has truly strayed from the Right Way

**THE QUESTION OF THE TWO MEN**

The reports give the asking its occasions, and they are three. Ibn Isḥāq carries from Ibn ʿAbbās, through Saʿīd ibn Jubayr or ʿIkrimah: Rāfiʿ ibn Ḥuraymilah and Wahb ibn Zayd said to the Messenger of God ﷺ: bring us a book you send down upon us from the heaven, that we may read it, and gush for us rivers, and we will follow you and affirm you! And God sent down, upon that saying of the two of them: or do you intend to ask of your messenger, as Mūsā was asked before. And al-Suddī read the asking by its oldest form: that He show them God openly; and the Arabs asked the Messenger of God ﷺ to bring them God, that they see Him openly. Mujāhid carries the asking of Quraysh: Quraysh asked Muḥammad ﷺ to make for them al-Ṣafā gold, and he said: yes! And it is for you, as the table of the Children of Israel, if you disbelieve! And they refused, and returned; and God sent down: or do you intend to ask of your messenger, as Mūsā was asked before. The three occasions meet in one standing: the asking that proposes its own conditions upon the revelation, and makes the belief the price of the granting.

**THE EXPIATIONS OF THE HOUSE**

Al-Rabīʿ carries, from Abū al-ʿĀliyah, an asking of another kind — the asking of the better: a man said: O Messenger of God, if only our expiations were the expiations of the Children of Israel! And the Prophet ﷺ said: O God, we do not desire it! What God gave you is better than what He gave the Children of Israel — the Children of Israel, when one of them did the sin, found it written upon his door, and its expiation; and if he expiated it, it was for him a disgrace in the world, and if he did not expiate it, it was for him a disgrace in the Hereafter; and God has given you better than what He gave the Children of Israel — (4:110 — **“Whoever commits evil or wrongs themselves then seeks Allah’s forgiveness will certainly find Allah All-Forgiving, Most Merciful”**) — and he said: the five prayers, and the Friday to the Friday, are expiations for what is between them. The asking of the verse is measured by this asking: the one asked that the burdens of the house be taken from the Book, and the other asked that the mercy of the Book be exchanged for its harder face — and the Prophet ﷺ refused the exchange for his community.

**THE ADAB OF THE ASKING AND THE MEANING OF THE OR**

Ibn Kathīr reads the verse as the sūrah's law of the asking: God forbade the believers, by this verse, the asking of the Messenger ﷺ the numerous questions, of the matters that had not occurred yet; and the like is His saying: (5:101 — **“Do not ask about any matter which, if made clear to you, may disturb you”**); and it is in the Ṣaḥīḥ: the greatest criminal of the Muslims is the one who asks about a thing that was not prohibited, and it becomes prohibited by his asking; and when the Messenger of God ﷺ was asked, of the husband who finds another man with his wife — if he exposes it, he exposes a great matter, and if he is silent, he is silent about a great matter — he disliked such questions, until God revealed the ruling of the liʿān; and when he told the Companions that God ordered them with the ḥajj, a man said: every year, O Messenger of God? And he did not answer him, and the man repeated it three times; and Anas ibn Mālik said: we were forbidden from asking the Messenger of God ﷺ about things, and we were delighted when a bedouin man came and asked him, while we listened.

Al-Ṭabarī reads the or by its meaning: the interpretation of the speech is: do you intend, O people, to ask of your messenger, of the things, the like of what the people of Mūsā asked of him, before you — and disbelieve, if you are refused it, in your asking of what is not permissible, in the wisdom of God, to give you; or perish, if it be of what is permissible, in His wisdom, to give you, and He give it to you, and you disbelieve after that, as perished who was before you, of the nations that asked their prophets what was not for them to ask of them, and when they were given, disbelieved, and were hastened by the punishments, for their disbelief, after the giving of God to them of their asking. The closing of the verse names the exchange that stands beneath the asking: the trading of the belief for the disbelief — and the straying of the trader is the straying from the right way itself.

---

## Verse 2:109

> Many among the People of the Book wish they could turn you ˹believers˺ back to disbelief because of their envy, after the truth has been made clear to them. Pardon and bear with them until Allah delivers His decision. Surely Allah is Most Capable of everything

**THE PASSAGE ADDRESSED TO THE BELIEVERS**

Al-Ṭabarī fixes the place of the verse in the passage: this saying of God makes plain that His address, in all these verses, from His saying, O believers, do not say rāʿinā — though the speech turns, in itself, to the address of the Prophet ﷺ — is only an address from Him to the believers of his Companions, and a reproach of them, and a forbidding of the sincere-counsel of the Jews and their likes of the people of association, and the accepting of their opinions in anything of the matters of their religion; and a proof that some of them had used, in the address of the Messenger of God ﷺ and the asking of him, the rudeness that the Jews used — for their Lord said to them, forbidding that: do not say to your prophet as the Jews say to him, rāʿinā, in imitation of them; but say, unẓurnā, and hear; for the harming of the Messenger of God ﷺ is disbelief in Me, and a denial of My right upon you, of his magnifying and his reverencing. The wishing of the verse is read upon that: it is the wishing of the many of the house, that the believers be returned, after their belief, disbelievers — the same returning the house wished upon the Messenger himself, in the days of his prayer for victory.

**THE TWO BROTHERS AND THE ENVY**

Ibn Isḥāq carries from Ibn ʿAbbās, through Saʿīd ibn Jubayr or ʿIkrimah: Ḥuyayy ibn Akhṭab and Abū Yāsir ibn Akhṭab were of the most intense of the Jews, in envy of the Arabs, when God singled them out with His Messenger ﷺ; and the two were striving in the turning of the people from Islam, as much as they were able; and God sent down, concerning the two of them: many of the People of the Book wish they could turn you back. Al-Ḍaḥḥāk carries from Ibn ʿAbbās the measure of the refusal: an unlettered messenger came to the People of the Scriptures, confirming what they have in their own Books, of the messengers and the signs of God, and he believes in all of that, as they believe in it — and they rejected the Prophet, out of disbelief, and envy, and transgression. And al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas said of His saying, of their own selves: it is of their making. The envy of the verse is thus the envy of the sending, not of the possession: it rises from the selves, after the truth has been made clear — and the clarity of the truth is what makes the envy a rebellion, and not a doubt.

**THE PARDON UNTIL THE COMMAND**

The verse then gives the community its standing with the envy: pardon and bear with them, until God delivers His decision. Al-Qurṭubī names the decision: He means, by His saying, until God comes with His command, the killing of Qurayẓah, and the expulsion of Banū l-Naḍīr. And the reports carry the verse its later standing: Ibn ʿAbbās said, in the report of ʿAlī ibn Abī Ṭalḥah: the pardon and the bearing were abrogated by the verse of the sword — by fight those who do not believe in Allah and the Last Day (9:29 — **“Fight those who do not believe in Allah and the Last Day”**); and the like was said by Abū al-ʿĀliyah, and al-Rabīʿ ibn Anas, and Qatādah, and al-Suddī. And Usāmah ibn Zayd carries the standing of the first days: the Messenger of God ﷺ and his Companions used to forgive the disbelievers and the People of the Book, as God commanded in His saying, pardon and bear with them. The verse carries, then, the two standings of the community with the envy of the house: the pardon that kept the peace, and the command that followed it — and each of the two was the standing of its time, until the command came.

---

## Verse 2:110

> Establish prayer, and pay alms-tax. Whatever good you send forth for yourselves, you will ˹certainly˺ find ˹its reward˺ with Allah. Surely Allah is All-Seeing of what you do

**THE OCCUPATION OF THE PRESENT**

The verse turns the community from the watching of the envy to the occupation of the hour. Al-Saʿdī reads the turning: God commanded the believers, after His telling of the envy of the many of the People of the Book, the occupying of the present time, with the establishing of the prayer, and the giving of the zakāh, and the doing of every act of nearness; and He promised them that whatever they do of good, it is not lost with God, but they find it with Him, full and preserved. Al-Alūsī reads the joining of the verse: the establishing of the prayer is joined upon the pardon of the verse before — as if God Most High commanded them with the opposition to the enviers, and the taking of refuge in Him, by the worship of the body and the wealth, for it repels from them what they dislike. Al-Ṭabarī states the reason of the command here: the establishing of the prayers is an expiation of the sins, and the giving of the zakāh is a purifying of the souls and the bodies from the stains of the offenses, and in the sending forward of the goods is the reaching of the winning, by the satisfaction of God; and he says the community was commanded here with that, for the annulling of what preceded of them, of the leaning to the saying of the Jews, and the rudeness of the one who was rude of them, in his address of the Messenger of God ﷺ, by his saying, rāʿinā.

**THE SENDING AND THE FINDING**

Al-Baghawī reads the sending by its faces: whatever you send forth, for yourselves, of good — of the obedience and the righteous act, you will find it with God; and it was said: He intended, by the good, the wealth, as in His saying, (2:180 — **“if they leave something of value”**) — He intended, of the zakāh, or the charity; and you will find it with God — even the date, and the morsel, like Uḥud. Al-Alūsī reads the finding by its idiom: you will find it with God — meaning, you will find its reward with Him; and the speech is upon the omitting of the added; and it was said: the apparent is that the meaning is, you find it in the knowledge of God Most High, and His establishing of it in His knowledge is in the place of its establishing with Him. And al-Rabīʿ said of His saying, you will find it: he means, you will find its reward, with God. Al-Ṭabarī gives the whole verse its standing: We have made plain, in what passed, the meaning of the establishing of the prayer, and that it is its performing, with its limits and its obligations; and God means by His saying, and whatever you send forward, for yourselves, of good, you will find it with God: whatever act you do, of the righteous, in the days of your life, and send forward, before your death, as a store for yourselves, in your returning, you will find its reward with your Lord, on the Day of Resurrection, and He will recompense you by it.

Al-Qurṭubī sets over the sending its poetry: the poet said — send forth, for yourself, before your death, a righteous work, and act, for there is not, to the vacancy, a way; and another said — send forth, for yourself, a repentance hoped for, before the death, and before the binding of the tongues; and Abū l-ʿAtāhiyah said — be ready, with your wealth, in your life; and fairest of it all is the saying: be, for yourself, an heir, if you are able — for the one who makes himself his heir is rightly guided. The closing of the verse sets the seeing over the sending: al-Ṭabarī reads: this is a report from God to the believers He addressed with these verses, that whatever they do of good and of evil, secretly and openly, He is seeing of it, and nothing of it is hidden from Him, and He will recompense them, by the good-doing, the better, and by the evil-doing, its like; and the speech, though it came out the going out of the report, carries in it the promise and the threat, and the command and the restraining; and baṣīr is mubṣir, turned to baṣīr, as mubdiʿ was turned to badīʿ, and muʾlim to alīm. The sending is done in the days of the life, and the finding is done on the Day of the return — and between the two stands the seeing of the One who does not lose a date or a morsel.
